A narrative inquiry approach to understanding senior intensive 
page=1 block=1445225850 (x=1 y=84) width=501 height=12 fontname=ONDBHQ+Lato-Bold wordint.min=4 wordint.max=6
care nurses’ experiences of working with new graduate nurses
page=1 block=1445225850 (x=1 y=107) width=494 height=12 fontname=ONDBHQ+Lato-Bold wordint.min=4 wordint.max=6
Nerilee Torning PhD, RN?|   Joanna Patching RN PhD, Associate Professor
page=1 block=912790599 (x=1 y=168) width=395 height=8 fontname=ONDBHQ+Lato-Bold wordint.min=3 wordint.max=5
Susan Whittam RN GradCertClinNurs (Critical Care), MEd (Adult Ed.), MPhil., CNE ?|   
page=1 block=1294058862 (x=1 y=150) width=465 height=8 fontname=ONDBHQ+Lato-Bold wordint.min=3 wordint.max=5
Aims and objectives: To report a qualitative inquiry exploring senior registered nurses’ 
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=229) width=342 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
stories of experience working with new graduate nurses in the intensive care unit.
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=244) width=328 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Background: While new graduate nurses’ perceptions of transitioning into profes-
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=259) width=340 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
sional practice in the intensive care unit have been examined, few studies have ex-
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=274) width=340 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
plored the experiences of the senior registered nurses working alongside them.
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=289) width=317 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Design: A  narrative inquiry  methodology  informed  by Dewey's (1938) theory of 
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=304) width=342 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
experience.
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=319) width=46 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=1 wordint.max=21
Methods: Individual in-depth, unstructured, interactive interviews were conducted 
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=334) width=342 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
with five senior registered nurses. The inquirer co-composed individual narrative ac-
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=349) width=340 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
counts with each participant. Narrative inquiry's three commonplaces of temporality, 
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=364) width=342 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
sociality and place formed the lens of analysis.
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=379) width=185 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Thematic analysis identified two overarching threads that resonated across the narra-
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=394) width=340 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tive accounts. This inquiry adheres to COREQ checklist reporting.
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=409) width=263 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Results: The overarching threads ‘Reverberations’ and ‘Caring’ depict the experiences 
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=424) width=342 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of senior registered nurses.  ‘It's Dangerous’, ‘Patrolling Like Surf Lifesavers’, ‘We 
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=439) width=342 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Carry Them’, ‘Survival Mode’ and ‘Enjoyable Moments’ are minor threads describing 
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=454) width=342 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the challenges, while ‘I’ve Been There’, ‘They Must Ask Questions’ and ‘Not In My 
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=469) width=342 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Backyard’ reveal their insights.
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=484) width=123 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Conclusions: Working with new graduate nurses increased senior registered nurses’ 
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=499) width=342 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
patient surveillance and workload, contributing to stress, pressure and feeling over-
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=514) width=340 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
whelmed. However, senior registered nurses felt an obligation to care for new gradu-
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=529) width=340 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ate nurses, who they perceived as still acquiring the skills to autonomously care for 
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=544) width=342 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
critically ill patients. Therefore, senior registered nurses require support themselves.
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=559) width=337 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Relevance to clinical practice: Senior registered nurses perceive workplace con-
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=574) width=340 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
straints as impeding their capacity to provide sufficient support and surveillance to 
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=589) width=342 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
new graduate nurses. The gap between new graduate nurses’ clinical capacity and 
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=604) width=342 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
patients’ needs underpins senior registered nurses’ recommendation that new gradu-
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=619) width=340 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ate nurses should not be placed in intensive care units unless additional workforce 
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=634) width=342 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
support is provided, safeguarding their development as well as patient safety.
page=1 block=1919592808 (x=158 y=649) width=309 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

1? |? INTRODUC TION
page=2 block=728236551 (x=1 y=109) width=118 height=7 fontname=LYRORW+Lato-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Senior registered nurses (SRNs) working in the intensive care unit (ICU) 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=137) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
have acquired the skills and knowledge to care for the most critically ill 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=150) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
patients. Historically, registered nurses (RNs) did not commence their 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=163) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
career in critical care areas of a hospital. Rather, they had one year 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=176) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of clinical experience in acute hospital wards which was thought to 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=189) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
provide a better foundation to enter the challenging ICU environment. 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=202) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
However, in 2009, a selection of new graduate nurses (NGNs) em-
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=215) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ployed by the inquiry site began their career in the ICU (Saghafi et al., 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=228) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
2012). The SRNs bear most of the responsibility for guiding NGNs’ 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=241) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
development, often in addition to their own clinical responsibilities. 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=254) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
As ICUs continue to experience staffing pressure from international 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=267) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
nursing shortages (Sawatzky et al., 2015), an inquiry into the working 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=280) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
relationship of SRNs towards NGNs was needed.
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=293) width=170 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The global nursing shortage can be explained as a supply and 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=14 y=306) width=231 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
demand problem. Demand for RNs continues to grow amid increas-
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=319) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ing patient numbers needing hospital-level health care. Contributing 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=332) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
factors include an increasing reliance on technologically advanced 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=345) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
health care plus the increased age and growth of the population 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=358) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
(Mar? et al., 2019). Concurrently, a worldwide under-supply and 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=371) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
predicated shortage of RNs can be explained by an ageing RN work-
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=384) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
force (Mar? et al., 2018), a reduction in nursing graduates, NGN at-
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=397) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
trition from hospital workforces (Hussein et al., 2019) and unhealthy 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=410) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
work environments (Ulrich et al., 2014).
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=423) width=141 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Areas such as ICUs may experience greater nursing shortages 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=14 y=436) width=231 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
(Sawatzky et al., 2015) due to onerous workloads, an ageing work-
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=449) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
force, attrition from the nursing workforce and high patient acuity 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=462) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
(Duffield et al., 2014). Caring for critically ill patients is demanding, 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=475) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
both physically and emotionally, evoking a greater propensity to 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=488) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
burnout than other clinical areas (American Association of Critical 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=501) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Care Nurses [AACN], 2016).
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=514) width=100 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Nursing workforce attrition negatively affects healthcare effi-
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=14 y=527) width=229 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ciency and cost, ultimately reducing the quality of patient health care 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=540) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
(Duffield et al., 2014). Nurses’ ability to deliver high-quality patient 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=553) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
care is affected by hours of practice, nursing skill mix and practice en-
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=566) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
vironment. These factors are associated with rates of adverse patient 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=579) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
events (Twigg et al., 2013). Those NGNs who are not supported on 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=592) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
an evidenced-based Transition to Professional Practice programme, 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=605) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
self-report more negative safety practices, errors, decreased overall 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=618) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
competence and increased stress (Spector et al., 2015). SRNs are a 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=631) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
vital NGN resource, providing clinical improvement opportunities, 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=644) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
advice, support and education (Bortolotto, 2015).
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=657) width=174 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Some  SRNs  may  become  discouraged  and frustrated  when 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=14 y=670) width=231 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
faced with the increased workload of repetitive NGN recruitment, 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=683) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
orientation and preceptorship cycles (Ballem & MacIntosh, 2014). 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=696) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
When SRNs are in short supply or are stressed or over-worked, 
page=2 block=1887991591 (x=1 y=709) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
What does this paper contribute to the wider 
page=2 block=1457157255 (x=279 y=127) width=185 height=7 fontname=ONDBHQ+Lato-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
global community?
page=2 block=1457157255 (x=279 y=140) width=76 height=7 fontname=ONDBHQ+Lato-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
• SRNs describe negative emotions such as frustration, 
page=2 block=1503546689 (x=279 y=157) width=211 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
stress and guilt when balancing ICU clinical responsibili-
page=2 block=1503546689 (x=288 y=170) width=200 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ties while supporting NGNs. These negative emotions 
page=2 block=1503546689 (x=288 y=183) width=202 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
result from the perceived risk to the provision of safe 
page=2 block=1503546689 (x=288 y=196) width=202 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and quality ICU patient care when increased SRN work-
page=2 block=1503546689 (x=288 y=209) width=200 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
loads impede their capacity to provide sufficient NGN 
page=2 block=1503546689 (x=288 y=222) width=202 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
supervision.
page=2 block=1503546689 (x=288 y=235) width=44 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=1 wordint.max=17
• Conversely, SRNs describe positive feelings  such  as 
page=2 block=1503546689 (x=279 y=248) width=211 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
satisfaction and pride when NGNs flourish. Committed 
page=2 block=1503546689 (x=288 y=261) width=202 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
to supporting NGNs’ clinical and professional develop-
page=2 block=1503546689 (x=288 y=274) width=200 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ment, SRNs recommend additional resources, time and 
page=2 block=1503546689 (x=288 y=287) width=202 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
supernumerary staff to provide adequate patient sur-
page=2 block=1503546689 (x=288 y=300) width=200 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
veillance, NGN guidance and continuing in situ clinical 
page=2 block=1503546689 (x=288 y=313) width=202 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
education.
page=2 block=1503546689 (x=288 y=326) width=38 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=1 wordint.max=17
dissatisfaction, burnout and high turnover can result (AACN, 2016). 
page=2 block=848102479 (x=262 y=371) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Baumberger-Henry (2012) reports that some SRNs interact nega-
page=2 block=848102479 (x=262 y=384) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tively with NGNs. Consequently, NGNs may become disheartened, 
page=2 block=848102479 (x=262 y=397) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
contributing to higher attrition rates. Reducing both SRN and NGN 
page=2 block=848102479 (x=262 y=410) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
turnover rates is an important goal to reduce the associated human 
page=2 block=848102479 (x=262 y=423) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and financial costs that affect healthcare budgets as well as patient 
page=2 block=848102479 (x=262 y=436) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and staff outcomes (Duffield et al., 2014).
page=2 block=848102479 (x=262 y=449) width=149 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
2?|?BACKGROUND
page=2 block=17792118 (x=262 y=486) width=110 height=7 fontname=LYRORW+Lato-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
A  systematised review process guided a  literature search from 
page=2 block=74735260 (x=262 y=514) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
January 2000–November 2018, with inclusion criteria of original 
page=2 block=74735260 (x=262 y=527) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
research studies reporting SRNs’ experiences or views of NGNs 
page=2 block=74735260 (x=262 y=540) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
working in ICU. Research not situated in ICU or specifically related 
page=2 block=74735260 (x=262 y=553) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
to NGNs’ or nursing students’ ICU experience was excluded. The

patient acuity and workload intensity. Studies exploring NGNs’ ex-

2009), a lack of confidence (Ortiz, 2016), unsupportive work envi-

and have a desire to learn (St Clair, 2013).

Descriptions of programmes designed to orientate NGNs to ICU

(Bortolotto, 2015; Friedman et al., 2011; Welding, 2011) revealed a

quality nursing care. However, the programmes did not investigate

page=3 block=1506648430 (x=1 y=475) width=174 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

SRNs’ perceptions of NGNs’ ICU experiences as the second phase

of a study (O'Kane, 2012) or included SRNs from an ICU alongside 
page=3 block=1506648430 (x=1 y=514) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
other hospital wards (Ballem & MacIntosh, 2014; Baumberger-Henry, 
page=3 block=1506648430 (x=1 y=527) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
2012). Although O’Kane’s (2012) study sought to explore the NGNs’ 
page=3 block=1506648430 (x=1 y=540) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

page=3 block=1506648430 (x=1 y=553) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

page=3 block=1506648430 (x=1 y=566) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

of the ICU well, the ICU and SRNs were placed under considerable 
page=3 block=1506648430 (x=1 y=592) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
pressure when supporting NGNs, raising concerns regarding the 
page=3 block=1506648430 (x=1 y=605) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
NGNs’ lack of hospital knowledge. The rate of preceptor burnout 
page=3 block=1506648430 (x=1 y=618) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
increased with the number of NGNs employed. The authors deter-
page=3 block=1506648430 (x=1 y=631) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
mined that NGNs require 8 weeks of supernumerary time and be 
page=3 block=1506648430 (x=1 y=644) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
supported by two nursing preceptors. Additionally, a focus on com-

pleting competency-based training was thought to contribute to cre-

ating a generation of task-oriented RNs (O’Kane, 2012).
page=3 block=1506648430 (x=1 y=683) width=198 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Ballem  and  MacIntosh’s  (2014)  study  sought  to  understand 
page=3 block=1506648430 (x=14 y=696) width=231 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
SRNs’ perspectives of working with NGNs in various hospital clinical 
page=3 block=1506648430 (x=1 y=709) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
settings, including critical care [ICU]. Analysis identified three themes: 
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=262 y=33) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
‘‘New Graduates Are Coming’, ‘Keeping Us on Our Toes’ and ‘Carrying 
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=262 y=46) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the Load’’ (p. 379). Despite a considerable workload increase when 
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=262 y=59) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
working with NGNs, SRNs were motivated to refresh and update 
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=262 y=72) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
their skills and knowledge; anticipating NGNs’ questions and need for 
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=262 y=85) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
procedural support. Although SRNs voiced verbal support for NGNs, 
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=262 y=98) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
they also expressed annoyance when NGNs did not follow advice.
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=262 y=111) width=232 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Baumberger-Henry’s (2012) study examined SRNs’ perspectives 
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=275 y=124) width=232 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
on work relationships with NGNs in emergency and critical care 
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=262 y=137) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
[ICU] units. The study determined the following themes: ‘‘Lacking 
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=262 y=150) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Confidence’ and ‘Gaining Acceptance in the Unit Culture’’ (p. 301). 
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=262 y=163) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The SRNs expressed frustration with NGNs’ fear, lack of confidence, 
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=262 y=176) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
deficiencies in basic skills, critical thinking and time management. 
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=262 y=189) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The second theme described SRNs’ uncivil behaviour towards NGNs, 
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=262 y=202) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
such as belittling or ignoring NGNs’ questions and using patronising 
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=262 y=215) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
language. The SRNs recognised their collective silence legitimised 
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=262 y=228) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the unsupportive behaviour. Baumberger-Henry (2012) emphasised 
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=262 y=241) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
that NGNs should be encouraged and nurtured to ask question with-
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=262 y=254) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
out fear of being perceived as burdensome.
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=262 y=267) width=155 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Finding scant literature, the necessity to examine the SRNs’ ex-
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=275 y=280) width=230 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
periences when working with NGNs in the ICU was evident. Analysis 
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=262 y=293) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of SRNs’ experiences may reveal interpersonal, cultural and work-
page=3 block=1574503358 (x=262 y=306) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
place factors influencing workplace relationships, staff well-being,

retention and ultimately patient safety.

4?|?DESIGN/METHODOLOGY
page=3 block=2120493960 (x=262 y=447) width=168 height=7 fontname=LYRORW+Lato-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Narrative inquiry  methodology recognises  stories of experience 
page=3 block=1798636297 (x=262 y=475) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
as a source of valuable understanding and knowledge (Clandinin & 
page=3 block=1798636297 (x=262 y=488) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Connelly, 2000). The term ‘narrative’ refers to the phenomenon of 
page=3 block=1798636297 (x=262 y=501) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the experience being studied and the method of inquiry for its study 
page=3 block=1798636297 (x=262 y=514) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
(Clandinin & Connelly, 2000). The three-dimensional space of narra-
page=3 block=1798636297 (x=262 y=527) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tive inquiry is informed by Dewey’s (1938) criteria of ‘experience— 
page=3 block=1798636297 (x=262 y=540) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
interaction and continuity enacted in situations’ (Clandinin, 2013, 
page=3 block=1798636297 (x=262 y=553) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
p. 12). The three commonplaces of narrative inquiry that delineate 
page=3 block=1798636297 (x=262 y=566) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the inquiry space dimensions are ‘temporality, sociality and place’ 
page=3 block=1798636297 (x=262 y=579) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
(Clandinin, 2013, p. 39). These are defined in Table 1 and were used 
page=3 block=1798636297 (x=262 y=592) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
to guide the exploration of SRNs’ stories of experience. The con-
page=3 block=1798636297 (x=262 y=605) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
solidated criteria for reporting qualitative studies (COREQ) 32-item 
page=3 block=1798636297 (x=262 y=618) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
checklist was used to report this inquiry (File S1).
page=3 block=1798636297 (x=262 y=631) width=175 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
4.1? |? Participants
page=3 block=2127481101 (x=262 y=668) width=90 height=7 fontname=LYRORW+Lato-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
An idiographic approach was used to purposively enrol five self- 
page=3 block=92862012 (x=262 y=696) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
selecting participants who  were willing to tell their stories of 
page=3 block=92862012 (x=262 y=709) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

4?|???
page=4 block=1517266286 (x=1 y=1) width=26 height=9 fontname=LYRORW+Lato-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=17
WHITTAM eT Al.
page=4 block=427939937 (x=453 y=3) width=52 height=4 fontname=GAGWEG+Lato-Regular-SC700 wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Experiences under examination exist in temporal transition, not one stand- 
page=4 block=294649085 (x=61 y=30) width=254 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
alone moment in time independent from its past and its future influence 
page=4 block=294649085 (x=73 y=40) width=245 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
(Clandinin, 2013). Narrative inquirers consider people, places, things and 
page=4 block=294649085 (x=73 y=51) width=246 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
events under exploration as having a past, present and future.
page=4 block=294649085 (x=73 y=61) width=209 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Social and personal conditions of both the inquirer and the inquiry participant 
page=4 block=1408549350 (x=61 y=76) width=264 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
effect the inquiry. Personal conditions are ‘feelings, hopes, desires, 
page=4 block=1408549350 (x=73 y=86) width=228 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
aesthetic reactions and moral dispositions’ (Connelly & Clandinin, 2006, 
page=4 block=1408549350 (x=73 y=97) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
p.480). Social conditions include an individual's context, the place, 
page=4 block=1408549350 (x=73 y=107) width=225 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
encircling forces and factors, existential conditions and people (Connelly & 
page=4 block=1408549350 (x=73 y=118) width=254 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Clandinin, 2006).
page=4 block=1408549350 (x=73 y=128) width=58 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The inquirer (SW) accounted for and acknowledged in the inquiry the pre- 
page=4 block=1408549350 (x=61 y=139) width=251 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
existing and ongoing professional relationship with participants.
page=4 block=1408549350 (x=73 y=149) width=216 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
One or a sequence of places is ‘the specific concrete, physical, and topological 
page=4 block=1947683363 (x=61 y=163) width=265 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
boundaries of place where the inquiry and events take place’ (Connelly & 
page=4 block=1947683363 (x=73 y=174) width=248 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Clandinin, 2006, p. 480). Narrative inquirers acknowledge and recognise 
page=4 block=1947683363 (x=73 y=184) width=246 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the impact and qualities of place on the inquiry, as, people, places and 
page=4 block=1947683363 (x=73 y=195) width=237 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
stories are intricately linked (Clandinin, 2013).
page=4 block=1947683363 (x=73 y=205) width=154 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Temporality
page=4 block=1665324560 (x=7 y=30) width=40 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

page=4 block=230991505 (x=7 y=76) width=28 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=1 wordint.max=17
Place
page=4 block=2018164030 (x=7 y=163) width=18 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=1 wordint.max=17
experience working with NGNs in the ICU. This enabled location 
page=4 block=918730310 (x=1 y=241) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of individual participant voices and stories of experience within the 
page=4 block=918730310 (x=1 y=254) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
three-dimensional inquiry space, while facilitating rigorous analy-
page=4 block=918730310 (x=1 y=267) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
sis of each participant's narrative account. The literature presents 
page=4 block=918730310 (x=1 y=280) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
minimal research determining qualitative sample sizes, although 
page=4 block=918730310 (x=1 y=293) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
recommendations exist. For example, when referring to narrative 
page=4 block=918730310 (x=1 y=306) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
research, Creswell and Poth (2016) recommend selecting and ob-
page=4 block=918730310 (x=1 y=319) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
serving one or more individuals who have stories and experiences 
page=4 block=918730310 (x=1 y=332) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
to share. It has been argued that a sample size of ‘one single case 
page=4 block=918730310 (x=1 y=345) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
can be highly informative and meaningful as demonstrated from 
page=4 block=918730310 (x=1 y=358) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
management and medical research’ (Boddy, 2016, p.426). Focussing 
page=4 block=918730310 (x=1 y=371) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
on narrative inquiry, Clandinin and Connelly (2000) provide exem-
page=4 block=918730310 (x=1 y=384) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
plars of inquires involving one, three and five participants.
page=4 block=918730310 (x=1 y=397) width=212 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The inquiry was conducted at a single site, public quaternary 
page=4 block=918730310 (x=14 y=410) width=231 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

4.2?|?Field Text [Data] collection
page=4 block=1175662879 (x=262 y=304) width=157 height=7 fontname=LYRORW+Lato-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The first author (SW) undertook unstructured interactive interviews 
page=4 block=1698746141 (x=262 y=332) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
between October 2016–January 2017, negotiating time and space 
page=4 block=1698746141 (x=262 y=345) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
for both inquirer and participant to tell and hear each other's stories 
page=4 block=1698746141 (x=262 y=358) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of experience (Clandinin, 2013). Playing an active role, the inquirer 
page=4 block=1698746141 (x=262 y=371) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
commenced with a ‘grand tour question’ (Spradley, 2016). Focused 
page=4 block=1698746141 (x=262 y=384) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
listening, probes and responding to participant questions were used 
page=4 block=1698746141 (x=262 y=397) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
to gain rich details regarding the phenomenon under inquiry. The 
page=4 block=1698746141 (x=262 y=410) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
interview guide is provided in Table 3.

pants; all chose a private location proximal to the ICU. The inter-

page=4 block=248050873 (x=262 y=564) width=134 height=7 fontname=LYRORW+Lato-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4

accounts and when referencing participant quotes.

page=5 block=1417941371 (x=478 y=1) width=27 height=9 fontname=LYRORW+Lato-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
WHITTAM eT Al.
page=5 block=937366737 (x=1 y=3) width=52 height=4 fontname=GAGWEG+Lato-Regular-SC700 wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Intensive care unit  The inquiry site ICU department contains two discrete specialities: cardiothoracic surgery and general/neuro/trauma. 
page=5 block=2130400175 (x=6 y=43) width=472 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Although registered nurses (RNs) are mainly allocated to one speciality with separate nursing rosters, they work 
page=5 block=2130400175 (x=92 y=53) width=378 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
throughout the department as dictated by staffing activity and clinical demand.
page=5 block=2130400175 (x=92 y=64) width=268 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
During weekday office hours, two clinical nurse consultants, a nurse educator, two clinical nurse educators (CNE) and 
page=5 block=2130400175 (x=80 y=74) width=397 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
two nurse unit managers (NUMs) support the ICU nursing workforce. When the NUMs have designated office time, 
page=5 block=2130400175 (x=92 y=85) width=391 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
a team leader (TL) is assigned to manage the clinical area. Customarily, the TL commences the shift without a patient 
page=5 block=2130400175 (x=92 y=95) width=393 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
load. However, if an unexpected urgent ICU admission occurs, in a context of lean staffing, the TL would take a patient 
page=5 block=2130400175 (x=92 y=106) width=401 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
allocation.
page=5 block=2130400175 (x=92 y=116) width=35 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=1 wordint.max=17
The nursing workforce in ICU is not augmented by additional healthcare specialists such as respiratory therapists or dialysis 
page=5 block=2130400175 (x=80 y=127) width=418 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
nurses. Commonly, one appropriately qualified RN problem solves and is responsible for all patient care of a critically ill 
page=5 block=2130400175 (x=92 y=137) width=402 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
patient, including the invasive and technological devices required to deliver organ support (Chamberlain et al., 2018). 
page=5 block=2130400175 (x=92 y=148) width=395 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
For example, permanently employed RNs independently manage patients requiring mechanical ventilation, including 
page=5 block=2130400175 (x=92 y=158) width=394 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
assessing patients’ ventilation requirements, adjusting ventilator settings and all aspects of maintaining an artificial 
page=5 block=2130400175 (x=92 y=169) width=388 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
airway. Other advanced technology overseen by RNs encompass extracorporeal membrane oxygenation (ECMO) 
page=5 block=2130400175 (x=92 y=179) width=382 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and ventricular assist devices (VADs), inhaled nitric oxide and intra-aortic balloon pumps. Additionally, appropriately 
page=5 block=2130400175 (x=92 y=190) width=392 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
educated and competent RNs assume nursing positions in emergency situations, such as open-chest resuscitation and 
page=5 block=2130400175 (x=92 y=200) width=399 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ECMO cardiopulmonary resuscitation.
page=5 block=2130400175 (x=92 y=211) width=129 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
After a two-day hospital-wide orientation programme, NGNs allocated to ICU on a Transition to Professional Practice 
page=5 block=1998876751 (x=80 y=225) width=398 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
programme undertake a three-day ICU induction. Led by CNEs, the induction comprises an ICU orientation, ICU 
page=5 block=1998876751 (x=92 y=235) width=379 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
nursing principles, ICU technical skill stations and socialisation. After induction, NGNs are supernumerary (surplus to 
page=5 block=1998876751 (x=92 y=246) width=395 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
staffing requirements), shadowing an experienced RN who assumes full responsibility for their assigned patient. ‘The 
page=5 block=1998876751 (x=92 y=256) width=395 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
supernumerary period comprises seven 8-hour morning shifts for “first rotation” NGNs and three 8-hour morning shifts 
page=5 block=1998876751 (x=92 y=267) width=405 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
for “second rotation” NGNs’ (Whittam, 2018, p. 7). Each rotation last six months. A ‘first rotation’ is the first of two 6- 
page=5 block=1998876751 (x=92 y=277) width=397 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
month placements and is the NGNs’ first clinical experience as an independent RN. A ‘second rotation’ NGN has worked 
page=5 block=1998876751 (x=92 y=288) width=406 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
in another clinical area for six months before working in ICU. During the supernumerary period, each NGN works 
page=5 block=1998876751 (x=92 y=298) width=383 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
alongside an experienced RN, assuming incremental accountability for their assigned patient. Post the supernumerary 
page=5 block=1998876751 (x=92 y=309) width=399 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
stage, NGNs are independently assigned an ICU patient.
page=5 block=1998876751 (x=92 y=319) width=189 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
To provide continuing NGN support, preceptors are assigned shift-by-shift to NGNs, for an additional 6 weeks. Through the 
page=5 block=1998876751 (x=80 y=330) width=418 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
preceptorship period, in addition to their preceptorship obligations, SRNs are allocated patients. At the conclusion of a 
page=5 block=1998876751 (x=92 y=340) width=401 height=6 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
6-month ICU placement, NGNs are expected to safely care for a mechanically ventilated patient.
page=5 block=1998876751 (x=92 y=351) width=325 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
NGNs are RNs within their first year of clinical practice, post-graduation.
page=5 block=64989209 (x=80 y=365) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The resource nurse supports other RNs with workload, break relief and education for less-experienced nurses. Allocation 
page=5 block=1290358063 (x=80 y=390) width=411 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of a resource nurse is decided by patient acuity and available staffing on a shift-by-shift basis, as an example, when 
page=5 block=1290358063 (x=92 y=400) width=388 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
more than one patient requires ECMO. The RN who has been designated as the resource nurse is not assigned a patient 
page=5 block=1290358063 (x=92 y=411) width=404 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
allocation and remains available to take an emergency admission; thus, they may cease the resource nurse role at any 
page=5 block=1290358063 (x=92 y=421) width=396 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
time during the shift.
page=5 block=1290358063 (x=92 y=432) width=71 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
SRNs are RNs working in the ICU with at least five years’ clinical nursing experience. All SRN participants in this inquiry 
page=5 block=1756039478 (x=80 y=446) width=403 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
were team leaders; four were clinical nurse specialists, and all had post-graduate qualifications.
page=5 block=1756039478 (x=92 y=456) width=320 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The 12-month Transition to Professional Practice programme for NGNs employed at the inquiry site is guided by a 
page=5 block=1020897892 (x=80 y=470) width=386 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
framework integrating clinical rotations, supernumerary time, a preceptorship model, regular appraisals, professional 
page=5 block=1020897892 (x=92 y=481) width=395 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
development days, mandatory education sessions and socialisation support.
page=5 block=1020897892 (x=92 y=491) width=256 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
TABLE 2?Inquiry term definitions/description.
page=5 block=1119371910 (x=1 y=26) width=175 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
(ICU)

page=5 block=910691117 (x=1 y=590) width=73 height=7 fontname=LYRORW+Lato-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Transcript accuracy was checked by reading the transcript while 
page=5 block=668152723 (x=1 y=618) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
concurrently  listening  to the  digital  recording;  this  process fa-
page=5 block=668152723 (x=1 y=631) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
cilitated  inquirer  (SW)  immersion  in  the text.  Attentive to  the 
page=5 block=668152723 (x=1 y=644) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
three-dimensional space, transcripts were arranged manually into 
page=5 block=668152723 (x=1 y=657) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
chronologically and/or thematically coherent stories (Mishler, 1995) 
page=5 block=668152723 (x=1 y=670) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
with minimal smoothing of participants’ quotes. The subsequent 
page=5 block=668152723 (x=1 y=683) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
reconstructed  stories  were  termed tentative narrative accounts 
page=5 block=668152723 (x=1 y=696) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
(Clandinin, 2013).
page=5 block=668152723 (x=1 y=709) width=62 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Individual participants received a hard copy of their tentative 
page=5 block=526981115 (x=275 y=540) width=232 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
narrative account, and all participants reflected, commented and ne-
page=5 block=526981115 (x=262 y=553) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
gotiated elements of their story with the inquirer (SW). Credibility 
page=5 block=526981115 (x=262 y=566) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
was established when participants confirmed that their final nar-
page=5 block=526981115 (x=262 y=579) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
rative account accurately represented their story. Each narrative 
page=5 block=526981115 (x=262 y=592) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
account was left ‘whole’, respecting and honouring the individual 
page=5 block=526981115 (x=262 y=605) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
voice and story of experience (Appendix S2). An audit trail was main-
page=5 block=526981115 (x=262 y=618) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tained, with field notes, transcripts, research reflections and record-
page=5 block=526981115 (x=262 y=631) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ings retained.
page=5 block=526981115 (x=262 y=644) width=48 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

6.1? |? It's Dangerous
page=7 block=1131710157 (x=1 y=161) width=100 height=7 fontname=LYRORW+Lato-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4

page=7 block=364266169 (x=1 y=189) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

page=11 block=979822206 (x=1 y=696) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
property development in a local neighbourhood that is considered a 
page=11 block=979822206 (x=1 y=709) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
good idea if it occurred elsewhere. Due to the gap between under-
page=11 block=1976870927 (x=262 y=33) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
graduate curriculum and the reality of ICU patient care, SRNs be-
page=11 block=1976870927 (x=262 y=46) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
lieved it would be better for NGN Transition to Professional Practice 
page=11 block=1976870927 (x=262 y=59) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
programme placement to be in a less critical clinical area:
page=11 block=1976870927 (x=262 y=72) width=203 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Because they’re fresh from the uni. They haven’t got 
page=11 block=1167522035 (x=288 y=98) width=193 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the skills. (Camilla, Line. 73–74, p.91)
page=11 block=1167522035 (x=288 y=111) width=130 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
I definitely think they should find out how a hospital 
page=11 block=214929887 (x=288 y=137) width=193 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
works and have a bit of respect for the patient that 
page=11 block=214929887 (x=288 y=150) width=193 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

[Kath, Line. 448–449] (Whittam, 2018, p. 94).

8? |? DISCUSSION
page=12 block=995381766 (x=262 y=83) width=99 height=7 fontname=LYRORW+Lato-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
As one part of the solution to the nursing shortage, NGNs are re-
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=111) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
cruited to work in ICUs. This inquiry sought to explore SRNs’ experi-
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=124) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ences working with NGNs in ICU. Knowing the critical and pressured 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=137) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
environment of ICU and holding the belief that NGNs are not ad-
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=150) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
equately prepared to nurse safely in this context, SRNs suggest that 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=163) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
NGNs gain initial experience elsewhere. The SRNs felt responsible 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=176) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
for patient safety yet perceive time and resource impediments to 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=189) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
their surveillance of NGNs. In this context, SRNs rely on NGNs’ initi-
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=202) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ative to seek help; however, SRNs suspect that NGNs’ reluctance to 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=215) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ask questions may indicate they are trying to conceal deficits in their 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=228) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
knowledge. Despite the perceived burden of NGNs in ICU, SRNs find 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=241) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
their contribution to NGN development rewarding.
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=254) width=182 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The ICU RN shortage has impelled ICUs to employ NGNs. It 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=275 y=267) width=232 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
is reported 23.3% of NGNs are employed in critical care areas in 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=280) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the USA after  registration (National Council of State Boards of 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=293) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Nursing [NCSBN], 2018). In this inquiry and other reports (Ballem 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=306) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
& MacIntosh, 2014; Baumberger-Henry, 2012; O'Kane, 2012), SRNs 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=319) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
raised  concerns regarding  the inadequate preparation of NGNs, 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=332) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
limiting their capacity to nurse high-acuity patients. Lack of ward 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=345) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
experience may hinder NGNs’ development in ICU (O'Kane, 2012). 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=358) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Experience in clinical areas with limited technology provides NGNs 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=371) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
with opportunities to embed comprehensive nursing assessment 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=384) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
skills:  ‘accountability, responsibility and  the  ability  to  prioritise’ 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=397) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
(O'Kane, 2012, p. 49). The finding of perceived NGN unprepared-
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=410) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ness aligns with other studies recommending that NGNs should 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=423) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
gain experience in ICU as a student (O'Kane, 2012). As higher acuity 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=436) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
patients are increasingly managed in generalist wards, graduating 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=449) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
nurses should have exposure to the knowledge and skill set neces-
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=462) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
sary to manage critical patients wherever they are located (Williams 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=475) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
& Palmer, 2014). However, providing ICU clinical placements for 
page=12 block=2008762581 (x=262 y=488) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
nursing students increases pressure on SRNs to deliver quality clin-

page=14 block=1845760645 (x=453 y=3) width=52 height=4 fontname=GAGWEG+Lato-Regular-SC700 wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
12–26 weeks duration (Bortolotto, 2015; Friedman et al., 2011). 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=33) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Additionally, NGN ICU supernumerary periods differ in the litera-
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=46) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ture, ranging from periods of 8-weeks (O'Kane, 2012) to an entire 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=59) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
3-month rotation  (Missen  et  al.,  2016).  We  recommend well- 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=72) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
resourced NGN ICU orientation and supernumerary programmes 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=85) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
that deliver sustained clinical supervision, graded entry to ICU 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=98) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
nursing responsibility, didactic education and realistic workloads. 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=111) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
There is some evidence that ICU induction programmes are cost- 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=124) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
effective in terms of reduced turnover and increased RN retention 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=137) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
(Friedman et al., 2011). Decreased nursing leadership appreciation 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=150) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of the benefits of well- esourced and extended NGN ICU orienta-
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=163) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tion and supernumerary programmes, as well as fiscal limitations, 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=176) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
may affect both SRNs’ and NGNs’ workloads; compromising the 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=189) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
capability to provide safe and effective patient care. Collaboration 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=202) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
with academic nursing faculty may help nursing educators and 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=215) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
managers develop and evaluate evidence-based programmes, with 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=228) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the dual function of increasing support for SRNs and NGNs while 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=241) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
expanding undergraduate curriculum content to include current 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=254) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
understanding of complex clinical areas such as ICU (Mårtensson 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=267) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
et al., 2013). Further research is needed on NGN ICU Transition 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=280) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
to Professional Practice programmes to emphasise both SRN and 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=293) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
NGN  experiences, establish the financial and educational out-
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=306) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
comes, and consider constraints when comparing ICUs (due to 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=319) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
variances in ICU nursing staffing, financial and health organisation 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=332) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
models, and patient cohorts).
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=345) width=107 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
We found that SRNs nurture NGNs, influenced by their own 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=14 y=358) width=231 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
early nursing experience (temporality), their commitment to the nurs-
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=371) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ing profession (sociality) and the satisfaction of contributing to NGN 
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=384) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
professional growth in the ICU context (place).
page=14 block=645777794 (x=1 y=397) width=165 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
8.1? |? Limitations
page=14 block=1134237170 (x=1 y=434) width=85 height=7 fontname=LYRORW+Lato-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Although the narrative inquiry sociality commonplace brings the re-
page=14 block=983834017 (x=1 y=462) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
lationship between the inquirer (SW) and SRN participants to the 
page=14 block=983834017 (x=1 y=475) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
forefront, this is a potential limitation via perceptions of status and 
page=14 block=983834017 (x=1 y=488) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
power impeding the inquirer's access to, and analysis of, SRNs’ ex-
page=14 block=983834017 (x=1 y=501) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
perience. However, reflexivity was practised in peer-debriefing re-
page=14 block=983834017 (x=1 y=514) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
search team meetings and documented in field notes throughout 
page=14 block=983834017 (x=1 y=527) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the inquiry.
page=14 block=983834017 (x=1 y=540) width=41 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The inquiry was conducted at a single site where a homoge-
page=14 block=983834017 (x=14 y=553) width=229 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
nous cohort of five participants self-selected; therefore, the ex-
page=14 block=983834017 (x=1 y=566) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
periences presented may not reflect those of SRNs in other ICUs 
page=14 block=983834017 (x=1 y=579) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and are not envisaged for generalisation. Narrative inquiry re-
page=14 block=983834017 (x=1 y=592) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
search texts are richly textured and represent the complex stories 
page=14 block=983834017 (x=1 y=605) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of experience lived and told by participants alongside the primary 
page=14 block=983834017 (x=1 y=618) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
inquirer; honouring NI’s ontology of experience. Enrolling a rep-
page=14 block=983834017 (x=1 y=631) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
resentative and larger sample of nurses to produce generalisable 
page=14 block=983834017 (x=1 y=644) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
themes would have a required a different methodology (Clandinin 
page=14 block=983834017 (x=1 y=657) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
& Connelly, 2000).
page=14 block=983834017 (x=1 y=670) width=68 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
To fulfil the requirements of this Higher Research Degree proj-
page=14 block=983834017 (x=14 y=683) width=229 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ect, milestones had to  be  reached within  pre-determined time-
page=14 block=983834017 (x=1 y=696) width=242 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
frames. This limited the capacity of the primary inquirer to hear and 
page=14 block=983834017 (x=1 y=709) width=244 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
be attentive to more than five participant stories regarding the phe-
page=14 block=1962586186 (x=262 y=33) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
nomenon under inquiry.
page=14 block=1962586186 (x=262 y=46) width=86 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The potential limitations are ameliorated, and trustworthiness 
page=14 block=1962586186 (x=275 y=59) width=232 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
enhanced, by presenting the  SRNs’ narrative accounts, allowing 
page=14 block=1962586186 (x=262 y=72) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
readers their own interpretations and application to other settings 
page=14 block=1962586186 (x=262 y=85) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
[sociality] (Appendix S2). The narrative accounts and threads pre-
page=14 block=1962586186 (x=262 y=98) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
sented in the findings highlight ‘the temporal, unfolding, contextual 
page=14 block=1962586186 (x=262 y=111) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
nature of the threads rather than the certainty of the threads as 
page=14 block=1962586186 (x=262 y=124) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
fixed, frozen, or context (life) independent’ (Clandinin, 2013, p.143).
page=14 block=1962586186 (x=262 y=137) width=241 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
9?|?CONCLUSION
page=14 block=1015236238 (x=262 y=174) width=105 height=7 fontname=LYRORW+Lato-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Although limiting ICU employment to RNs with acute nursing 
page=14 block=657252457 (x=262 y=202) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
experience may not be possible in the current healthcare en-
page=14 block=657252457 (x=262 y=215) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
vironment, there is an essential need to generate and maintain 
page=14 block=657252457 (x=262 y=228) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
healthy  ICU  workplaces.  Therefore,  we  recommend  reducing 
page=14 block=657252457 (x=262 y=241) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
SRNs’ workloads when they are supporting NGNs and develop-
page=14 block=657252457 (x=262 y=254) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ing evidence-based, designed, evaluated and well-resourced NGN 
page=14 block=657252457 (x=262 y=267) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ICU orientation and supernumerary programmes. Furthermore, a 
page=14 block=657252457 (x=262 y=280) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
combination of managerial recognition, access to academic col-
page=14 block=657252457 (x=262 y=293) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
laboration and education that enhance SRNs’ education capacity 
page=14 block=657252457 (x=262 y=306) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and staffing that reflects total patient acuity may better support 
page=14 block=657252457 (x=262 y=319) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
both the SRNs and NGNs.
page=14 block=657252457 (x=262 y=332) width=96 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
This inquiry adds to existing nursing knowledge by making visible 
page=14 block=657252457 (x=275 y=345) width=232 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the SRNs’ ICU experience. ‘Readers of this current inquiry are in-
page=14 block=657252457 (x=262 y=358) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
vited to immerse themselves in the three-dimensional space; open-
page=14 block=657252457 (x=262 y=371) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ing up new possibilities for different stories of SRNs’ experiences 
page=14 block=657252457 (x=262 y=384) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
with NGNs in the ICU to be lived and told’ (Whittam, 2018, p. 121).
page=14 block=657252457 (x=262 y=397) width=240 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
10? |? RELE VANCE TO CLINIC A L PRACTICE
page=14 block=1031780408 (x=262 y=434) width=236 height=7 fontname=LYRORW+Lato-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
The findings of this inquiry have implications for policy decisions 
page=14 block=472262461 (x=262 y=462) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
regarding NGN placement in ICU on a Transition to Professional 
page=14 block=472262461 (x=262 y=475) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Practice programme. The NGNs enter the high-acuity, unpredict-
page=14 block=472262461 (x=262 y=488) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
able ICU environment with a recognised gap between their existing 
page=14 block=472262461 (x=262 y=501) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
clinical competence and the nursing requirements of ICU patients. 
page=14 block=472262461 (x=262 y=514) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Regarding ICU patient safety and quality of care, SRNs serve an es-
page=14 block=472262461 (x=262 y=527) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
sential role by providing surveillance, supervision and support while 
page=14 block=472262461 (x=262 y=540) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
NGNs are delivering nursing care. However, SRNs perceive that they 
page=14 block=472262461 (x=262 y=553) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
do not have enough resources to adequately support NGNs develop 
page=14 block=472262461 (x=262 y=566) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
their clinical competence in the ICU, which may have implications 
page=14 block=472262461 (x=262 y=579) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
for the quality of patient care provided. Although there may be fi-
page=14 block=472262461 (x=262 y=592) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
nancial implications for ICUs and healthcare organisations, staffing 
page=14 block=472262461 (x=262 y=605) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
decisions should be informed by the match between the total clinical 
page=14 block=472262461 (x=262 y=618) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
competence of the ICU nursing team and the needs of ICU patients, 
page=14 block=472262461 (x=262 y=631) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
to achieve best patient outcomes while sustaining healthy work en-
page=14 block=472262461 (x=262 y=644) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
vironments. The clinical capacity of NGNs at the start of their career, 
page=14 block=472262461 (x=262 y=657) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
SRNs’ workloads and ICU nursing staffing levels, is important fac-
page=14 block=472262461 (x=262 y=670) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tors to consider when designing and evaluating NGNs ICU induction 
page=14 block=472262461 (x=262 y=683) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and supernumerary programmes. Through SRNs’ stories of experi-
page=14 block=472262461 (x=262 y=696) width=243 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ence, this inquiry has provided an understanding of the ramifications 
page=14 block=472262461 (x=262 y=709) width=245 height=5 fontname=GYIKZG+Lato-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
